\frontierreportpart{Beyond the Dyadic Fabius Web}
% Absorbed from beyond_dyadic_fabius_web_report/fabius_frontier_report.tex




\begin{abstract}
The dyadic Fabius function, its inverse, Rvachev's compactly supported up-function,
and the signed Thue--Morse extension form an unusually dense network of exact identities.
The inspected repository already develops that network at considerable depth: exact dyadic
values, Gaussian-binomial coefficient extraction, Bernoulli and Bell polynomial formulas,
Lambert-$W$ endpoint phases, geometric Lagrange interpolation, Richardson filters, and an
extensive campaign on the decay of the infinite sinc product.  This report therefore does
not attempt another rearrangement of those results.  Instead it moves in a transverse
direction by replacing the dyadic ratio $1/2$ by a free geometric parameter $0<q<1$.

For independent $U_j\sim\mathrm{Unif}(0,1)$, define
\[
 X_q=(1-q)\sum_{j\ge0}q^jU_j,
 \qquad F_q(x)=\Probability(X_q\le x),
 \qquad Y_q=2X_q-1.
\]
At $q=1/2$ this is exactly the bounded Fabius distribution, and the density of $Y_{1/2}$
is Rvachev's up-function.  For the full family we prove a refinement equation and a
rapidly decaying infinite sinc product; exact Bernoulli cumulants and Bell moments; a
Bernoulli--Appell polynomial inverse to geometric uniform averaging; a finite-stage
$q$-binomial scale identity; monotone contraction in convex order; an exact shape phase
transition with a central plateau precisely when $q<1/2$; a Gaussian degeneration as
$q\uparrow1$ with explicit standardized cumulants; a two-term logarithmic endpoint law
\[
 \log F_q(x)
 =-\frac{\log^2(1/x)}{2\log(1/q)}
  -\frac{\log(1/x)\log\log(1/x)}{\log(1/q)}
  +\BigO_q(\log(1/x));
\]
and its inverse Lambert-$W$ quantile law.  We also determine Fourier-zero multiplicities
for reciprocal-integer ratios, isolate the arithmetic condition for cross-scale collisions,
and explain why base two alone converts the finite product into a contiguous Thue--Morse
block.  Finally, we give a phase-preserving Lagrange/Richardson filter for asymptotic
remainders whose coefficients are periodic in a lower-Lambert phase, together with a
Fourier phase-averaging filter and exact Lambert-$W$ node construction.

\pagebreak[4]
The weighted-law, CDF, and affine fixed-point portion of the post-snapshot Lean audit,
checked at compiled source checkpoint
\path{d312c0603}, now supplies the generic continuous-linear naturality,
conditional operator/scalar affine-recursion, real absolute-continuity, and exact-support
engines for arbitrary norm-summable weighted uniform series.  It also defines this report's
geometric-\(q\) weights, series, law, CDF, and explicit positive-ratio density.  For
\(|q|<1\), the real CDF is continuous, obeys reflection, and takes value \(1/2\) at the
midpoint.  For \(0\le q<1\), its exterior tails are exactly zero and one; for \(0<q<1\),
the two conditioning integrals, pointwise derivative formula, Radon--Nikodym density,
density reflection and compact support, and \(C^\infty\) regularity of both CDF and density
are formal.  At \(q=1/2\), exact all-real declarations identify the CDF with the bounded
Fabius function and the density with the affine Rvachev up-function.  A new generic
affine-independent-copy API proves probability fixed-point uniqueness on every complete
second-countable real inner-product target for a measurable digit map and \(|q|<1\), and its
geometric corollary characterizes this report's law even at \(q=0\) and negative \(q\), with
no support, density, or moment assumptions.

A later mainline tranche, rechecked in the
current combined full-facade build, gives the phase-bearing sinc prefixes an exact finite
residual formula and proves their pointwise convergence to the characteristic function for
every \(|q|<1\).  The latest analytic tranche now closes the corresponding named product
bridge.  For \(q,z\in\ComplexNumbers\), \(\lVert q\rVert<1\), write \(S_q(z)\) for
\path{geometricSincProduct} applied to \(q,z\).  Then
\(S_q(z)=\prod_{n\ge0}\SincRad(\pi q^nz)\) has locally uniform
product convergence, genuine pointwise multipliability and \path{HasProd}, and is entire, by
\path{hasProdLocallyUniformly_geometricSincProduct},
\path{geometricSincProductFactors_multipliable},
\path{hasProd_geometricSincProduct}, and
\path{geometricSincProduct_differentiable}.  For real \(|q|<1\), including \(q=0\) and
negative contractions, and \(t\in\RealNumbers\), putting \(z_q(t)=(1-q)t/(2\pi)\) gives the exact formula
\(\phi_q(t)=\EulerE^{\ImaginaryUnit t/2}S_q(z_q(t))=\EulerE^{\ImaginaryUnit t/2}G_q(z_q(t))G_q(-z_q(t))\), where
\(G_q(z)\) denotes \path{geometricReciprocalGamma} applied to \(q,z\), through
\path{charFun_geometricUniformDistribution_eq_phase_mul_geometricSincProduct} and
\path{charFun_geometricUniformDistribution_eq_phase_mul_geometricReciprocalGamma}.
Moreover, \path{tendstoLocallyUniformly_prefix_sinc_charFun} proves that the displayed
phase-bearing finite prefixes converge locally uniformly on the real frequency line to
\(\phi_q\), and \path{tendstoUniformlyOn_prefix_sinc_charFun} gives the explicit uniform
wrapper on every compact subset of \(\RealNumbers\).  Independently, for every nonzero such \(q\),
direct named Cauchy-transform and adjacent-power refinement equations hold off the exact
support.  A named centered wrapper, the report's
rapid-decay and inversion proof, explicit Bernoulli-cumulant/Bell-moment formulas and
asymptotics, further transforms, higher-derivative and endpoint-flatness wrappers, and shape
theory remain outside that promotion.

The post-snapshot activation tranche is a separate real, totalized hyperbolic kernel rather
than an identification with the complex sinc product.  It proves the sharp local coefficient
\(p(x)/x^2\to1/3\), the finite \(\tanh\) and activation Taylor jets through the displayed
orders, the arbitrary-index square-summable activation budget and its sharp Tannery limit,
and the geometric and dyadic effective-dimension specializations.  The positive-weight
active-count interpretation remains restricted to \(0<q<1\); the formal geometric activation
identities are deterministic and include negative \(q\), with no Bernoulli-family or
expectation bridge claimed.

The word \emph{new} is used only in a corpus-relative sense: these statements were not found
in the 57 TeX sources inspected in the specified repository snapshot.  No claim of global
priority is made.  Proven statements are separated sharply from conjectural transseries,
strict log-concavity, Edgeworth, inverse-phase, and arithmetic-collision problems.
Deterministic numerical experiments and all plotting code accompany the report.
\end{abstract}

\begin{boxedremark}
\textbf{Status convention.}
A theorem or proposition in this report is accompanied by an argument intended to be
mathematically complete at the paper level.  A conjecture is explicitly labeled and is not
asserted as proved.  None of the new statements has been formalized in Lean as part of the
repository snapshot.  The principal novelty filter was comparison against all 57 TeX files
listed in \texttt{corpus\_inventory.txt}, including frozen versions, the living syntheses,
the Thue--Morse atlas, all included paper sources, and the twelve Fourier-decay drafts and
audits.

Since that pinned snapshot, and as checked at compiled source checkpoint
\path{d312c0603}, \path{WeightedUniformSeries.lean},
\path{WeightedUniformDistribution.lean}, and \path{WeightedUniformSupport.lean} have
formalized the reusable product space, absolutely summable Banach-valued uniform-coordinate
series, and their pushforward laws, including the eleven-declaration generic naturality and
operator-affine recursion tranche, the compatibility API, the four-declaration real
absolute-continuity tranche, and the ten-declaration exact-support tranche.  In particular,
support equals the series range in a complete Borel real normed space, arbitrary signed real
weights have the exact interval between the sums of their negative and positive parts, and
every nontrivial norm-summable real weighted law is absolutely continuous with null
singletons.  The specialization
\path{GeometricUniformLaw.lean} defines
$w_j(q)=(1-q)q^j$ and $X_q$ for every real $q$, proves absolute convergence and total mass
one for $|q|<1$, and establishes the independent-copy affine refinement, law reflection,
absolute continuity, null singletons, and support equal to the exact series range.  For
$0\le q<1$ it proves that the range and support are exactly $[0,1]$, including $q=0$; at
$q=1/2$,
\path{ProbabilityRepresentation.lean} identifies both the random series and its law with
the classical dyadic construction and proves its exact support is $[0,1]$.

The later modules \path{ContinuousCDF.lean} and
\path{GeometricUniformCDF.lean} now define the named real CDF and explicit density and
formalize the precise parameter ranges stated in the abstract: continuity, reflection, and
the midpoint value for \(|q|<1\); exterior CDF values for \(0\le q<1\); and, for
\(0<q<1\), conditioning in subtype and ordinary interval-integral form, an everywhere
pointwise derivative, the resulting Radon--Nikodym identity, density symmetry and compact
support, and \(C^\infty\) regularity.  \path{ProbabilityRepresentation.lean} supplies the
all-real \(q=1/2\) Fabius-CDF and Rvachev-density bridges.
\end{boxedremark}

\begin{boxedremark}
\textbf{Post-snapshot formal status (continued).}

The new module \path{AffineIndependentCopy.lean} adds one definition and eight theorems:
the affine independent-copy law, probability preservation, its equality with the direct
product pushforward, the one-step characteristic-function factorization and fixed-point
recurrence, its finite iterate, uniqueness from a common contractive characteristic-function
recurrence, and uniqueness in convolution and product-map forms.  The uniqueness scope is
an arbitrary complete second-countable real inner-product target with its Borel structure,
an arbitrary probability digit space and measurable digit map, probability candidate laws,
and a real scalar satisfying \(|q|<1\).  It includes \(q=0\) and negative \(q\), and assumes
no support, absolute continuity, density, or moments.  The corollary
\path{eq_geometricUniformDistribution_of_selfSimilar} in
\path{GeometricUniformUniqueness.lean} therefore characterizes the named geometric-uniform
law by its displayed affine independent-copy equation throughout that full parameter range.

The definition of \path{geometricUniformDensity} is total, and the formal API now diagnoses
its nonpositive-parameter failure exactly: it is zero at \(q=0\), nonpositive for \(q<0\),
and its \path{ENNReal.ofReal}-clamped \path{withDensity} measure is zero, hence not the
geometric-uniform probability law when \(|q|<1\).  Lean formalization of the paper-level
corrected signed-density replacement for \(q\le0\) remains open.  Fixed-point uniqueness is
now the direct theorem
\path{eq_geometricUniformDistribution_of_selfSimilar}, valid for every
\(|q|<1\), including negative \(q\) and \(q=0\).  The definitions
\path{geometricUniformStieltjesDomain},
\path{geometricUniformStieltjesTransform}, and
\path{geometricUniformStieltjesPower} are total in real \(q\); for \(|q|<1\), the transform
is holomorphic off the exact support, and for \(q\ne0\) the direct named theorems
\path{hasDerivAt_geometricUniformStieltjesTransform_refinement} and
\path{geometricUniformStieltjesPower_succ} give the transform DDE and every positive
adjacent-power recurrence, including negative ratios.  At \(q=1/2\), the all-power affine
bridge, named unit Stieltjes DDE, and both canonical complex-slit adjacent-order recurrences
are direct theorems of \path{CauchyTransform.lean}.

The finite characteristic-function residual formula and its pointwise prefix limit are now
formal, and the product target is no longer merely a total \path{tprod}.  For
\(q,z\in\ComplexNumbers\) with \(\lVert q\rVert<1\), the four declarations
\path{hasProdLocallyUniformly_geometricSincProduct},
\path{geometricSincProductFactors_multipliable},
\path{hasProd_geometricSincProduct}, and
\path{geometricSincProduct_differentiable} prove locally uniform convergence of
\(S_q(z)=\prod_{n\ge0}\SincRad(\pi q^nz)\), its genuine pointwise product, and entire-ness.
For real \(|q|<1\), \(t\in\RealNumbers\), and \(z_q(t)=(1-q)t/(2\pi)\), including \(q=0\) and negative
ratios, \path{charFun_geometricUniformDistribution_eq_phase_mul_geometricSincProduct} and
\path{charFun_geometricUniformDistribution_eq_phase_mul_geometricReciprocalGamma} give
\[
 \phi_q(t)=\EulerE^{\ImaginaryUnit t/2}S_q(z_q(t))
 =\EulerE^{\ImaginaryUnit t/2}G_q(z_q(t))G_q(-z_q(t)).
\]
Here \(G_q(z)\) denotes \path{geometricReciprocalGamma} applied to \(q,z\).
The additional declarations \path{tendstoLocallyUniformly_prefix_sinc_charFun} and
\path{tendstoUniformlyOn_prefix_sinc_charFun} upgrade the same real-frequency prefixes to
local uniform convergence and uniform convergence on every compact subset of \(\RealNumbers\).
Still frontier are a named centered wrapper, the rapid-decay bound and Fourier
inversion route, a closed higher-derivative refinement and endpoint-flatness wrapper, the
shape transition, further transforms, and explicit Bernoulli-cumulant/Bell-moment formulas
and asymptotics.  The geometric tail dictionary already gives finite characteristic-function,
MGF, and CGF tail factorizations.  The closed density hierarchy here is distinct from the
proved Cauchy-power hierarchy.
\end{boxedremark}


\newpage

\section{Corpus audit and the novelty boundary}
\label{p10:sec:corpus}

\subsection{What was inspected}

The requested tree contains four logically different kinds of material.
First are the living synthesis documents: the primary Fabius--Rvachev exposition, the
mathematical glossary, the Lean walkthrough, the non-elementarity article, the consolidated
frontier volume, and the living Thue--Morse formula atlas.  Second is a twelve-document
Fourier-decay campaign containing proposed analyses, final syntheses, a gentle guide, and two
comparative audits.  Third is a seven-source paper library, including both the original and
corrected TeX of one OCR conversion.  Fourth is a frozen archive of thirty-two historical
versions organized by provenance and topic.  The resulting count is
\[
 6+12+7+32=57.
\]
The exact paths are reproduced in \Cref{p10:app:inventory} and in the companion plain-text
ledger.  The repository's own archive README states that byte-identical historical copies
were pruned and that the archive is grouped by purpose rather than serving as a naive mirror
of every earlier path \cite{p10:proveitArchive}.  Thus the historical sources were treated as a
novelty/provenance map, while the current synthesis documents were treated as the canonical
statement of what the project already contains.

\subsection{What the repository already does very well}

The current primary exposition and frontier synthesis already cover, among much else,
\begin{itemize}
\item the probabilistic construction of the Fabius distribution and the Rvachev bump;
\item exact dyadic evaluation, recurrences, bit-recursive algorithms, and denominator
      arithmetic;
\item Gaussian-binomial and $q$-Pochhammer structures at the fixed base $q=1/2$;
\item Bernoulli cumulants, Bell-polynomial moments, Appell-type blocks, and exact coefficient
      extraction;
\item geometric-node Lagrange identities and $q$-Richardson cancellation of geometric error
      modes;
\item lower-Lambert endpoint variables, nonconstant periodic correction functions, and
      all-orders saddle machinery;
\item the inverse Fabius function, totalization conventions, and dyadic inverse formulas;
\item repeated-integration and box-spline approximants;
\item extensive Thue--Morse identities, rarefied sums, Mahler equations, natural-boundary
      phenomena, and quantitative approximation questions;
\item the quadratic logarithmic envelope, exceptional zeros, and several amplitude notions
      for the Fourier image of the up-function.
\end{itemize}
These features are visible in the primary exposition \cite{p10:proveitPrimary}, the consolidated
frontier volume \cite{p10:proveitFrontiers}, the living atlas \cite{p10:proveitAtlas}, and the
Fourier audit campaign \cite{p10:proveitFourierAudit}.  The fixed-base $q$-calculus is especially
important for the present report: the archive explicitly describes those documents as
\emph{base-$1/2$} articles \cite{p10:proveitArchive}.  Likewise, the archive records an existing
``phase locking'' mechanism for dyadic coefficient extraction.  Accordingly, neither
Gaussian-binomial notation nor phase locking by itself is claimed as new below.

\subsection{Deliberate exclusions}

Three tempting projects were rejected because they would duplicate the corpus too closely.

\begin{enumerate}
\item We do not offer a thirteenth derivation of the leading Fourier decay
\(
 \log|\FourierAngular{\RvachevUp}(t)|\asymp-(\log|t|)^2/(2\log2)
\)
with the known zero-set and limsup qualifications.  The live Fourier drafts compare precisely
such arguments.
\item We do not repackage the exact dyadic value web, the half-base Gaussian-binomial
interpolation formula, or the existing geometric Richardson filter.
\item We do not relabel the existing dyadic lower-Lambert periodic asymptotic as a new
``transseries.''  The new phase-aware extrapolation theorem in \Cref{p10:sec:phase} applies to a
different numerical geometry: inverse powers of a Lambert variable while its fractional
phase is held fixed.
\end{enumerate}

\subsection{The transverse direction}

The central observation is that the dyadic distribution is one member of a natural geometric
one-parameter family.  Making the ratio variable simultaneously exposes:
\begin{itemize}
\item a shape transition at the dyadic value $q=1/2$;
\item a majorization order in the parameter;
\item a Gaussian limit as the geometric mesh becomes diffuse;
\item a general Bernoulli--Bell calculus whose dyadic specialization recovers the repository's
      formulas exactly;
\item a change in Fourier-zero arithmetic from generic simple zeros to valuation-controlled
      multiplicities;
\item a precise reason that the Thue--Morse block is special to base two.
\end{itemize}
This parameter deformation is the organizing principle of the report.

\section{The geometric \texorpdfstring{$q$}{q}-Fabius--Rvachev family}
\label{p10:sec:family}

Throughout, fix
\[
 0<q<1,
 \qquad c=1-q,
 \qquad a=\log(1/q)>0.
\]
Let $(U_j)_{j\ge0}$ be independent random variables, each uniform on $[0,1]$.

\begin{definition}[Geometric Fabius law]
\label{p10:def:qfabius}
Define
\begin{equation}
 X_q=c\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}q^jU_j,
 \qquad
 Z_q=X_q-\frac12,
 \qquad
 Y_q=2Z_q=2X_q-1.                         \label{p10:eq:q-law}
\end{equation}
Write $F_q$ for the extended cumulative distribution function of $X_q$,
$f_q$ for its density, and $u_q$ for the density of $Y_q$.
\end{definition}

Because $0\le U_j\le1$ and $c\sum q^j=1$, the series converges absolutely almost surely and
$X_q\in[0,1]$.  Replacing every $U_j$ by $1-U_j$ shows
\begin{equation}
 X_q\overset d=1-X_q,
 \qquad F_q(x)=1-F_q(1-x),
 \qquad f_q(x)=f_q(1-x).                   \label{p10:eq:symmetry}
\end{equation}
The support is all of $[0,1]$: every open subinterval of $[0,1]$ receives positive
probability by constraining finitely many $U_j$ and leaving a sufficiently short tail free.

\paragraph{Current formalization boundary.}
The declarations \path{hasSum_geometricUniformWeight},
\path{geometricUniformSeries_mem_Icc}, and
\path{geometricUniformDistribution_Icc} formalize the series, its unit total weight, and
concentration on $[0,1]$.  The stronger assertion in the preceding sentence is now captured
by \path{geometricUniformDistribution_support_eq_Icc}: exact topological support is
$[0,1]$.  The positive-mass formulation follows from the standard characterization of
support, rather than from a separately named interval-mass theorem.  More generally,
\path{geometricUniformDistribution_support_eq_range} identifies support with the exact
series range for every $|q|<1$.  Likewise
\path{geometricUniformDistribution_reflection} proves
$X_q\overset d=1-X_q$.  The measure is absolutely continuous by
\path{geometricUniformDistribution_absolutelyContinuous}, and
\path{geometricUniformDistribution_nullSingletonClass} packages its null singletons, both
for every \(|q|<1\) without a positivity hypothesis.  The generic theorem
\path{cdf_reflection_sub} and its specialization
\path{geometricUniformCDF_reflection} now package the displayed CDF symmetry on all of
\(\RealNumbers\), and \path{geometricUniformCDF_one_half} gives the midpoint value, again for
every \(|q|<1\).  On \(0\le q<1\),
\path{geometricUniformCDF_zero_of_nonpos} and
\path{geometricUniformCDF_one_of_one_le} give the exact exterior values.  For \(0<q<1\),
\path{geometricUniformDensity_reflection} packages the displayed density symmetry for the
explicit density \path{geometricUniformDensity}; its Radon--Nikodym interpretation and
regularity are recorded in the crosswalk below.  Independently of those positive-ratio
density results, \path{eq_geometricUniformDistribution_of_selfSimilar} proves that the
affine independent-copy equation uniquely determines the probability law for every
\(|q|<1\), including \(q=0\) and negative \(q\), without any support, density, or moment
hypothesis on the competing measure.

\paragraph{Exact public API crosswalk.}
The one-definition, eight-theorem contractive layer in

They define
\[
 T_{\rho,d,q}(\mu)=d_*\rho\ast(q\,\cdot)_*\mu,
\]
identify it with the pushforward of \(\rho\otimes\mu\) by
\((a,x)\mapsto d(a)+qx\), prove probability preservation and characteristic-function
factorization, iterate the fixed-point recurrence to \(q^n t\), and prove uniqueness.
The final uniqueness theorems assume a complete second-countable real inner-product target
with its Borel structure, a probability digit space with measurable digit map, probability
candidate laws, and \(|q|<1\); they need no support, absolute-continuity, density, or moment
hypothesis and include \(q=0\) and negative contractions.

The remaining weighted-series names in this crosswalk lie in the namespace

 \phi_q(t)=e^{\ImaginaryUnit(1-q^m)t/2}

 \phi_q(q^m t),
\]
and the phase-bearing prefix without the residual converges pointwise to \(\phi_q(t)\).

 S_q(z)=\prod_{n\ge0}\SincRad(\pi q^nz)

 \phi_q(t)=\EulerE^{\ImaginaryUnit t/2}S_q(z_q(t))
